Oh, well... I suppose I do have a little bit of a routine, just to make sure I'm presentable. I wouldn't call it anything too extreme, I promise! 

Just... before a training class at work, or when I'm helping out at the senior-center workshops, I might pop into the ladies' room to check the mirror. Maybe just a few times, just to be absolutely sure. I'll put some lotion on my hands so they don't look so dry, and... well, I might turn my face a few different ways in the light, just to see how the angles look and check on that unevenness. Sometimes I'll even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little peace of mind. 

I'll admit it does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes, which I always feel terrible about, but I still get myself there and get the job done! I just like to make sure I look neat for everyone. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor? I hope I don't sound too vain admitting all that.

But then... well, like I said, the other ladies say they don't see it at all. So I suppose maybe I am making a bit too big a deal out of it? \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm losing my sense of perspective or anything like that. I just... I don't know for sure. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too foolish saying all this.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly—like checking mirrors, trying to cover them up, or going over it in your mind—to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do have a little bit of a routine, just to make sure I'm presentable. I wouldn't call it anything too extreme, I promise! \n\nJust... before a training class at work, or when I'm helping out at the senior-center workshops, I might pop into the ladies' room to check the mirror. Maybe just a few times, just to be absolutely sure. I'll put some lotion on my hands so they don't look so dry, and... well, I might turn my face a few different ways in the light, just to see how the angles look and check on that unevenness. Sometimes I'll even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little peace of mind. \n\nI'll admit it does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes, which I always feel terrible about, but I still get myself there and get the job done! I just like to make sure I look neat for everyone. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor? I hope I don't sound too vain admitting all that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have good insight into these concerns about your hands and face, meaning you recognize that your worries might be more intense or frequent than what most people experience?\n\nPatient: Well, um... insight? I'm not entirely sure what you mean by that, doctor. I mean, I try very hard to be reasonable about things. I really do. \n\nI know I worry about it... probably a little more than the other ladies do. Like I said, they tell me they don't see what I see. But to me, it's just so clear when I look down at my hands or see those pictures. So, I suppose I realize that maybe my... my worrying about it is a bit much? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and looking professional for the safety classes. \n\nI don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality or anything like that. I just... I want to look nice. Does that answer your question? I hope I'm giving you the right information. I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about your hands and face ever make you feel noticeably upset, embarrassed, or distressed — maybe even to the point where it’s hard to enjoy things or be yourself around others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm completely miserable or anything like that. I really try to keep a positive attitude! I definitely haven't let it stop me from doing my job or helping out at the senior center. I'd never want to let anyone down. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does bother me a little bit. Like I mentioned, it makes me a little slower getting ready for the training classes, because I'm just trying to make sure I look okay. And, um... lately, I've found myself sort of stepping out of the way when people bring out a camera. I just... Well, um... I certainly wouldn't say I do things over and over in a bad way. I try very hard not to be a bother to anyone or make a fuss! But... I suppose when I'm getting ready, especially right before a safety class or one of my workshops at the senior center, I do like to make sure I'm presentable.

I hope that doesn't sound too vain or silly of me. It does make me run a little bit behind schedule sometimes, just taking those extra minutes to double-check, but I always get myself there and get the job done! I'd never let it ruin the class or anything like that.

I suppose I do feel a little self-conscious, yes. I mean, it's not like I'm having a panic attack up there or anything dramatic like that! I don't want to make it sound worse than it is. It's just... I guess I worry a little bit that people might be looking at my hands and thinking I look too old to be doing the trainings, or that they're noticing how crooked my face looks in those pictures. \n\nBut I always make sure to smile and get through the presentation just fine. I really try not to let it show, because I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm not doing a good job. I hope that's a normal thing to feel? I mean, nobody likes having their picture taken, right? I'm probably just being a bit vain, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do these concerns about your hands and face distract you or take up a lot of your time, to the point where it interferes with your daily life or causes you a lot of upset?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no, it certainly hasn't stopped me from doing my job or volunteering! I would never let it get in the way of my responsibilities. \n\nBut... well, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, it does slow me down a little bit when I'm getting ready for the trainings. I just spend a bit more time trying to make sure I look presentable, you know? That's normal for a woman my age, right? \n\nThough, I guess I have started sort of... quietly ducking out of the way when they bring the camera around at work. Just so I don't ruin the picture. And sometimes I catch myself hesitating before going to social get-togethers that I used to just jump right into and enjoy. I just feel a bit shy about it all lately, I suppose. It's really not a huge disruption, I promise, I still get everything done. I hope that's the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about how you see these concerns about your hands and face? And then my mind just sort of wanders to those newsletter photos, and I start worrying about my face looking crooked again. It's silly, isn't it? Especially since the other ladies swear they don't see it. I just can't seem to shake the thought, though. I try to just push it away and focus on my work, but it keeps popping back up. Is it normal to get stuck on something like that? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these concerns about your hands and face are excessive or unreasonable, even though you try not to let them bother you too much?\n\nPatient: Well, um... excessive? I'm not really sure. I certainly hope I'm not being unreasonable! But then... well, like I said, the other ladies say they don't see it at all. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too foolish saying all this.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly—like checking mirrors, trying to cover them up, or going over it in your mind—to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do have a little bit of a routine, just to make sure I'm presentable. And, um... lately, I've found myself sort of stepping out of the way when people bring out a camera. I just... I don't want to ruin the picture with my face or my hands, you know? \n\nAnd I guess I do hesitate a tiny bit before going to some of the social get-togethers I used to really look forward to. I still go! I just... I worry a little more about how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly insecure or silly. I just like to put my best foot forward, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do your worries about your appearance ever go beyond your hands and face, or do they mostly stay focused on those specific areas?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, doctor, goodness no. It's really just my hands and my face, like we talked about. I definitely don't sit around nitpicking my whole body or anything like that! I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely obsessed with myself. \n\nIt's just... well, working on the production floor all these years, your hands just take a bit of a beating, you know? And your face is what everyone sees first when you're talking to them, especially when I'm up there doing the safety training. So those are the only things I really fuss over, just to make sure I look neat and tidy for everyone. I really don't worry about the rest of it at all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything over and over — like checking mirrors, covering them up, or going through the details in your head — to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I certainly wouldn't say I do things over and over in a bad way. I try very hard not to be a bother to anyone or make a fuss! But... I suppose when I'm getting ready, especially right before a safety class or one of my workshops at the senior center, I do like to make sure I'm presentable. \n\nSo I might, you know, just pop into the bathroom and check the mirror a few times. And I'll put some extra lotion on my hands, and maybe turn my face a few different ways in the light just to see how everything looks. Sometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little reassurance. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too vain or silly of me. It does make me run a little bit behind schedule sometimes, just taking those extra minutes to double-check, but I always get myself there and get the job done! I'd never let it ruin the class or anything like that. \n\nDoes that sound like what you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly and giving you what you need.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you tell me if these concerns about your hands and face have ever caused you to avoid certain situations, or made it harder for you to function in your usual roles at work, at home, or with friends?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 64 / production_worker / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"It hasn’t stopped me from working or volunteering, but I’m slower getting ready for trainings and I’ve started ducking out of photos or hesitating before social events I used to enjoy more easily.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well, um... I know I touched on this a little bit before, but I really want to assure you that I haven't let it stop me from doing my job or my volunteering! I take my responsibilities very seriously, and I would never want to leave anyone in a lurch. 

It's just... well, like I said, it does make me a bit slower getting ready for my training classes. I just need those extra few minutes to make sure everything is... presentable. And I suppose I have started ducking out of photos lately. I just don't want to spoil anyone's nice memories with my hands or my face looking the way they do. 

And, um, I do find myself hesitating a bit more before going to social events. Things I used to just go to without a second thought... now I just pause and worry a little about how I'll look to everyone else. But I still go! I really try my best not to let it hold me back completely. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not coping well. I really am trying my best to keep up with everything. Does that answer your question, doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information.

I really try not to let it show, because I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm not doing a good job. I hope that's a normal thing to feel? I mean, nobody likes having their picture taken, right? I'm probably just being a bit vain, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do these concerns about your hands and face distract you or take up a lot of your time, to the point where it interferes with your daily life or causes you a lot of upset?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no, it certainly hasn't stopped me from doing my job or volunteering! I would never let it get in the way of my responsibilities. \n\nBut... well, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, it does slow me down a little bit when I'm getting ready for the trainings. I just spend a bit more time trying to make sure I look presentable, you know? That's normal for a woman my age, right? \n\nThough, I guess I have started sort of... quietly ducking out of the way when they bring the camera around at work. Just so I don't ruin the picture. And sometimes I catch myself hesitating before going to social get-togethers that I used to just jump right into and enjoy. I just feel a bit shy about it all lately, I suppose. It's really not a huge disruption, I promise, I still get everything done. I hope that's the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about how you see these concerns about your hands and face? Do you find yourself thinking about them often during the day, even when you're not in front of a mirror or about to take a photo?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do think about it a fair bit. I really don't want to sound like I'm obsessed or anything, because I know there are much more important things going on in the world. But... yes, ever since I started helping with those safety trainings, it's just sort of... always there in the back of my mind. \n\nEven when I'm just sitting at my station or driving home, I catch myself thinking about how wrinkly and uneven my hands must have looked when I was pointing at the safety charts. And then my mind just sort of wanders to those newsletter photos, and I start worrying about my face looking crooked again. It's silly, isn't it? Especially since the other ladies swear they don't see it. I just can't seem to shake the thought, though. I try to just push it away and focus on my work, but it keeps popping back up. Is it normal to get stuck on something like that? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these concerns about your hands and face are excessive or unreasonable, even though you try not to let them bother you too much?\n\nPatient: Well, um... excessive? I'm not really sure. I certainly hope I'm not being unreasonable! I really try hard not to be one of those people who makes a mountain out of a molehill. \n\nI mean, I know I shouldn't worry about it as much as I do, and it's probably just me being a little silly. Because I do see the wrinkles and the crookedness, you know? It looks very real to me when I look at those pictures or look down at my hands. But then... well, like I said, the other ladies say they don't see it at all. So I suppose maybe I am making a bit too big a deal out of it? \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm losing my sense of perspective or anything like that. I just... I don't know for sure. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too foolish saying all this.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly—like checking mirrors, trying to cover them up, or going over it in your mind—to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do have a little bit of a routine, just to make sure I'm presentable. I wouldn't call it anything too extreme, I promise! \n\nJust... before a training class at work, or when I'm helping out at the senior-center workshops, I might pop into the ladies' room to check the mirror. Maybe just a few times, just to be absolutely sure. I'll put some lotion on my hands so they don't look so dry, and... well, I might turn my face a few different ways in the light, just to see how the angles look and check on that unevenness. Sometimes I'll even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little peace of mind. \n\nI'll admit it does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes, which I always feel terrible about, but I still get myself there and get the job done! I just like to make sure I look neat for everyone. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor? I hope I don't sound too vain admitting all that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have good insight into these concerns about your hands and face, meaning you recognize that your worries might be more intense or frequent than what most people experience?\n\nPatient: Well, um... insight? I'm not entirely sure what you mean by that, doctor. I mean, I try very hard to be reasonable about things. I really do. \n\nI know I worry about it... probably a little more than the other ladies do. Like I said, they tell me they don't see what I see. But to me, it's just so clear when I look down at my hands or see those pictures. So, I suppose I realize that maybe my... my worrying about it is a bit much? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and looking professional for the safety classes. \n\nI don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality or anything like that. I just... I want to look nice. Does that answer your question? I hope I'm giving you the right information. I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about your hands and face ever make you feel noticeably upset, embarrassed, or distressed — maybe even to the point where it’s hard to enjoy things or be yourself around others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm completely miserable or anything like that. I really try to keep a positive attitude! I definitely haven't let it stop me from doing my job or helping out at the senior center. I'd never want to let anyone down. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does bother me a little bit. Like I mentioned, it makes me a little slower getting ready for the training classes, because I'm just trying to make sure I look okay. And, um... lately, I've found myself sort of stepping out of the way when people bring out a camera. I just... I don't want to ruin the picture with my face or my hands, you know? \n\nAnd I guess I do hesitate a tiny bit before going to some of the social get-togethers I used to really look forward to. I still go! I just... I worry a little more about how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly insecure or silly. I just like to put my best foot forward, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do your worries about your appearance ever go beyond your hands and face, or do they mostly stay focused on those specific areas?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, doctor, goodness no. It's really just my hands and my face, like we talked about. I definitely don't sit around nitpicking my whole body or anything like that! I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely obsessed with myself. \n\nIt's just... well, working on the production floor all these years, your hands just take a bit of a beating, you know? And your face is what everyone sees first when you're talking to them, especially when I'm up there doing the safety training. So those are the only things I really fuss over, just to make sure I look neat and tidy for everyone. I really don't worry about the rest of it at all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything over and over — like checking mirrors, covering them up, or going through the details in your head — to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I certainly wouldn't say I do things over and over in a bad way. I try very hard not to be a bother to anyone or make a fuss! But... I suppose when I'm getting ready, especially right before a safety class or one of my workshops at the senior center, I do like to make sure I'm presentable. \n\nSo I might, you know, just pop into the bathroom and check the mirror a few times. And I'll put some extra lotion on my hands, and maybe turn my face a few different ways in the light just to see how everything looks. Sometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little reassurance. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too vain or silly of me. It does make me run a little bit behind schedule sometimes, just taking those extra minutes to double-check, but I always get myself there and get the job done! I'd never let it ruin the class or anything like that. \n\nDoes that sound like what you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if these concerns about your hands and face have ever caused you to avoid certain situations, or made it harder for you to function in your usual roles at work, at home, or with friends?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, um... I know I touched on this a little bit before, but I really want to assure you that I haven't let it stop me from doing my job or my volunteering! I take my responsibilities very seriously, and I would never want to leave anyone in a lurch. \n\nIt's just... well, like I said, it does make me a bit slower getting ready for my training classes. I just need those extra few minutes to make sure everything is... presentable. And I suppose I have started ducking out of photos lately. I just don't want to spoil anyone's nice memories with my hands or my face looking the way they do. \n\nAnd, um, I do find myself hesitating a bit more before going to social events. Things I used to just go to without a second thought... now I just pause and worry a little about how I'll look to everyone else. But I still go! I really try my best not to let it hold me back completely. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not coping well. I really am trying my best to keep up with everything. Does that answer your question, doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the worry about your hands or face made it hard for you to interact with others, or caused you to feel so upset that it was difficult to enjoy being around people?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really wouldn't say it's ever gotten quite that bad. I mean, I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting in a corner somewhere, unable to talk to anyone or just miserable all the time! \n\nI really do enjoy being around people, especially my friends at the senior center and the folks at work. Like I mentioned, I might feel a little... hesitant sometimes, just worrying if they're noticing my hands or if my face looks alright in the lighting. But once I'm there and we get to chatting, I still have a lovely time. I would never let something silly like this ruin a nice get-together or make me rude to anyone. \n\nI just try to focus on being a good listener and doing my part, you know? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for. I just want to be honest with you without making it sound like I'm falling apart, because I'm really not! Am I making sense, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when your concern about how your hands or face look made you feel so upset that it was hard to focus on what you were doing, or made you wish you could just stay home and avoid being around people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I really don't think so. I mean, I certainly wouldn't say I get so upset that I can't concentrate. That sounds awful, doesn't it? I really try to just keep my mind on my work or whatever task is at hand. \n\nAnd as for wishing I could just stay home... well, maybe a fleeting thought now and then if I'm having a particularly bad morning, but I would never actually do it! I'd feel just terrible letting people down like that. I always make sure I get out the door and get to where I need to be, even if I have to put on a brave face. \n\nI really don't want to sound like a complainer or someone who just gives up. I hope I'm explaining this right... I just want to make sure you know I'm still functioning and doing my part! Is that helpful?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That's normal for a woman my age, right? \n\nThough, I guess I have started sort of... quietly ducking out of the way when they bring the camera around at work. Just so I don't ruin the picture. And sometimes I catch myself hesitating before going to social get-togethers that I used to just jump right into and enjoy. I just feel a bit shy about it all lately, I suppose. It's really not a huge disruption, I promise, I still get everything done. I hope that's the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about how you see these concerns about your hands and face? Do you find yourself thinking about them often during the day, even when you're not in front of a mirror or about to take a photo?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do think about it a fair bit. I really don't want to sound like I'm obsessed or anything, because I know there are much more important things going on in the world. But... yes, ever since I started helping with those safety trainings, it's just sort of... always there in the back of my mind. \n\nEven when I'm just sitting at my station or driving home, I catch myself thinking about how wrinkly and uneven my hands must have looked when I was pointing at the safety charts. And then my mind just sort of wanders to those newsletter photos, and I start worrying about my face looking crooked again. It's silly, isn't it? Especially since the other ladies swear they don't see it. I just can't seem to shake the thought, though. I try to just push it away and focus on my work, but it keeps popping back up. Is it normal to get stuck on something like that? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these concerns about your hands and face are excessive or unreasonable, even though you try not to let them bother you too much?\n\nPatient: Well, um... excessive? I'm not really sure. I certainly hope I'm not being unreasonable! I really try hard not to be one of those people who makes a mountain out of a molehill. \n\nI mean, I know I shouldn't worry about it as much as I do, and it's probably just me being a little silly. Because I do see the wrinkles and the crookedness, you know? It looks very real to me when I look at those pictures or look down at my hands. But then... well, like I said, the other ladies say they don't see it at all. So I suppose maybe I am making a bit too big a deal out of it? \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm losing my sense of perspective or anything like that. I just... I don't know for sure. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too foolish saying all this.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly—like checking mirrors, trying to cover them up, or going over it in your mind—to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do have a little bit of a routine, just to make sure I'm presentable. I wouldn't call it anything too extreme, I promise! \n\nJust... before a training class at work, or when I'm helping out at the senior-center workshops, I might pop into the ladies' room to check the mirror. Maybe just a few times, just to be absolutely sure. I'll put some lotion on my hands so they don't look so dry, and... well, I might turn my face a few different ways in the light, just to see how the angles look and check on that unevenness. Sometimes I'll even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little peace of mind. \n\nI'll admit it does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes, which I always feel terrible about, but I still get myself there and get the job done! I just like to make sure I look neat for everyone. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor? I'm not entirely sure what you mean by that, doctor. I mean, I try very hard to be reasonable about things. I really do. \n\nI know I worry about it... probably a little more than the other ladies do. Like I said, they tell me they don't see what I see. But to me, it's just so clear when I look down at my hands or see those pictures. So, I suppose I realize that maybe my... my worrying about it is a bit much? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and looking professional for the safety classes. \n\nI don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality or anything like that. I just... I want to look nice. Does that answer your question? I hope I'm giving you the right information. I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about your hands and face ever make you feel noticeably upset, embarrassed, or distressed — maybe even to the point where it’s hard to enjoy things or be yourself around others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm completely miserable or anything like that. I really try to keep a positive attitude! I definitely haven't let it stop me from doing my job or helping out at the senior center. I'd never want to let anyone down. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does bother me a little bit. I just like to put my best foot forward, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do your worries about your appearance ever go beyond your hands and face, or do they mostly stay focused on those specific areas?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, doctor, goodness no. I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything over and over — like checking mirrors, covering them up, or going through the details in your head — to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I certainly wouldn't say I do things over and over in a bad way. I try very hard not to be a bother to anyone or make a fuss! But... I suppose when I'm getting ready, especially right before a safety class or one of my workshops at the senior center, I do like to make sure I'm presentable. \n\nSo I might, you know, just pop into the bathroom and check the mirror a few times. And I'll put some extra lotion on my hands, and maybe turn my face a few different ways in the light just to see how everything looks. I hope that's the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about how you see these concerns about your hands and face? Do you find yourself thinking about them often during the day, even when you're not in front of a mirror or about to take a photo?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do think about it a fair bit. I really don't want to sound like I'm obsessed or anything, because I know there are much more important things going on in the world. But... yes, ever since I started helping with those safety trainings, it's just sort of... always there in the back of my mind. \n\nEven when I'm just sitting at my station or driving home, I catch myself thinking about how wrinkly and uneven my hands must have looked when I was pointing at the safety charts. And then my mind just sort of wanders to those newsletter photos, and I start worrying about my face looking crooked again. It's silly, isn't it? Especially since the other ladies swear they don't see it. I just can't seem to shake the thought, though. I try to just push it away and focus on my work, but it keeps popping back up. Is it normal to get stuck on something like that? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these concerns about your hands and face are excessive or unreasonable, even though you try not to let them bother you too much?\n\nPatient: Well, um... excessive? I'm not really sure. I certainly hope I'm not being unreasonable! I really try hard not to be one of those people who makes a mountain out of a molehill. \n\nI mean, I know I shouldn't worry about it as much as I do, and it's probably just me being a little silly. Because I do see the wrinkles and the crookedness, you know? It looks very real to me when I look at those pictures or look down at my hands. But then... well, like I said, the other ladies say they don't see it at all. So I suppose maybe I am making a bit too big a deal out of it? \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm losing my sense of perspective or anything like that. I just... I don't know for sure. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too foolish saying all this.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly—like checking mirrors, trying to cover them up, or going over it in your mind—to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do have a little bit of a routine, just to make sure I'm presentable. I wouldn't call it anything too extreme, I promise! \n\nJust... before a training class at work, or when I'm helping out at the senior-center workshops, I might pop into the ladies' room to check the mirror. Maybe just a few times, just to be absolutely sure. I'll put some lotion on my hands so they don't look so dry, and... well, I might turn my face a few different ways in the light, just to see how the angles look and check on that unevenness. Sometimes I'll even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little peace of mind. \n\nI'll admit it does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes, which I always feel terrible about, but I still get myself there and get the job done! I just like to make sure I look neat for everyone. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor? I hope I don't sound too vain admitting all that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have good insight into these concerns about your hands and face, meaning you recognize that your worries might be more intense or frequent than what most people experience?\n\nPatient: Well, um... insight? I'm not entirely sure what you mean by that, doctor. I mean, I try very hard to be reasonable about things. I really do. \n\nI know I worry about it... probably a little more than the other ladies do. Like I said, they tell me they don't see what I see. But to me, it's just so clear when I look down at my hands or see those pictures. So, I suppose I realize that maybe my... my worrying about it is a bit much? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and looking professional for the safety classes. \n\nI don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality or anything like that. I just... I want to look nice. Does that answer your question? I hope I'm giving you the right information. I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about your hands and face ever make you feel noticeably upset, embarrassed, or distressed — maybe even to the point where it’s hard to enjoy things or be yourself around others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm completely miserable or anything like that. I really try to keep a positive attitude! I definitely haven't let it stop me from doing my job or helping out at the senior center. I'd never want to let anyone down. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does bother me a little bit. Like I mentioned, it makes me a little slower getting ready for the training classes, because I'm just trying to make sure I look okay. And, um... lately, I've found myself sort of stepping out of the way when people bring out a camera. I just... I don't want to ruin the picture with my face or my hands, you know? \n\nAnd I guess I do hesitate a tiny bit before going to some of the social get-togethers I used to really look forward to. I still go! I just... I worry a little more about how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly insecure or silly. I just like to put my best foot forward, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do your worries about your appearance ever go beyond your hands and face, or do they mostly stay focused on those specific areas?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, doctor, goodness no. It's really just my hands and my face, like we talked about. I definitely don't sit around nitpicking my whole body or anything like that! I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely obsessed with myself. \n\nIt's just... well, working on the production floor all these years, your hands just take a bit of a beating, you know? And your face is what everyone sees first when you're talking to them, especially when I'm up there doing the safety training. So those are the only things I really fuss over, just to make sure I look neat and tidy for everyone. I really don't worry about the rest of it at all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything over and over — like checking mirrors, covering them up, or going through the details in your head — to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I certainly wouldn't say I do things over and over in a bad way. I try very hard not to be a bother to anyone or make a fuss! But... I suppose when I'm getting ready, especially right before a safety class or one of my workshops at the senior center, I do like to make sure I'm presentable. \n\nSo I might, you know, just pop into the bathroom and check the mirror a few times. And I'll put some extra lotion on my hands, and maybe turn my face a few different ways in the light just to see how everything looks. Sometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little reassurance. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too vain or silly of me. It does make me run a little bit behind schedule sometimes, just taking those extra minutes to double-check, but I always get myself there and get the job done! I'd never let it ruin the class or anything like that. \n\nDoes that sound like what you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if these concerns about your hands and face have ever caused you to avoid certain situations, or made it harder for you to function in your usual roles at work, at home, or with friends?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, um... I know I touched on this a little bit before, but I really want to assure you that I haven't let it stop me from doing my job or my volunteering! I take my responsibilities very seriously, and I would never want to leave anyone in a lurch. \n\nIt's just... well, like I said, it does make me a bit slower getting ready for my training classes. I just need those extra few minutes to make sure everything is... presentable. And I suppose I have started ducking out of photos lately. I just don't want to spoil anyone's nice memories with my hands or my face looking the way they do. \n\nAnd, um, I do find myself hesitating a bit more before going to social events. Things I used to just go to without a second thought... now I just pause and worry a little about how I'll look to everyone else. But I still go! I really try my best not to let it hold me back completely. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not coping well. I really am trying my best to keep up with everything. Does that answer your question, doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the worry about your hands or face made it hard for you to interact with others, or caused you to feel so upset that it was difficult to enjoy being around people?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really wouldn't say it's ever gotten quite that bad. I mean, I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting in a corner somewhere, unable to talk to anyone or just miserable all the time! \n\nI really do enjoy being around people, especially my friends at the senior center and the folks at work. Like I mentioned, I might feel a little... hesitant sometimes, just worrying if they're noticing my hands or if my face looks alright in the lighting. But once I'm there and we get to chatting, I still have a lovely time. I would never let something silly like this ruin a nice get-together or make me rude to anyone. \n\nI just try to focus on being a good listener and doing my part, you know? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for. I just want to be honest with you without making it sound like I'm falling apart, because I'm really not! Am I making sense, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when your concern about how your hands or face look made you feel so upset that it was hard to focus on what you were doing, or made you wish you could just stay home and avoid being around people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I really don't think so. I mean, I certainly wouldn't say I get so upset that I can't concentrate. That sounds awful, doesn't it? I really try to just keep my mind on my work or whatever task is at hand. \n\nAnd as for wishing I could just stay home... well, maybe a fleeting thought now and then if I'm having a particularly bad morning, but I would never actually do it! I'd feel just terrible letting people down like that. I always make sure I get out the door and get to where I need to be, even if I have to put on a brave face. \n\nI really don't want to sound like a complainer or someone who just gives up. I hope I'm explaining this right... I just want to make sure you know I'm still functioning and doing my part! Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so distressed about how your hands or face look that it made it hard for you to function normally, such as at work, in social settings, or in other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Well, um, I know we've touched on this a bit already, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much... but I want to be absolutely sure I'm answering you correctly. \n\nI haven't let it stop me from working or doing my volunteering at the senior center. I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I'd hate for you to think I'm the sort of person who just stops functioning. \n\nBut... well, like I mentioned, I suppose it has made me a little slower when I'm getting ready for my training classes. I just need to make sure everything looks as nice as it can. And I have been trying to stay out of photographs lately, just so I don't ruin them. I also find myself pausing and hesitating a bit before going to social things that I used to just go to without a second thought. \n\nI still go, though! I promise I'm still doing all the things I need to do. I really hope that makes sense, doctor. Am I giving you the right kind of information?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your concerns about how your hands or face look have ever caused you such significant distress that it was difficult to focus or function normally, even for a short time?\n\nPatient: Well, I feel terrible because I know you just asked me this, and I really don't want to seem like I'm not listening or being difficult! I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know. \n\nI really haven't let it stop me from doing my job on the production line or helping out at the senior center. I still do all of that, I promise. It's just... well, maybe it has made things a tiny bit harder in small ways? Like I said, I do take a bit longer getting ready for my training classes now, just trying to make sure I look presentable so I don't distract anyone. And I have been ducking out of photos lately, and maybe hesitating a little before going to social things that I used to just jump right into. \n\nBut I'm still functioning, I really am! I just... I suppose it takes a little more effort sometimes. I hope I'm not sounding too repetitive or like I'm making a fuss over nothing. Does that help clarify things, doctor?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Oh, yes... well, that's actually a big part of why I decided to come in. I hope it's okay to bring this up now. 

It's just that... well, since I started helping out with the safety trainings at work, I just can't seem to stop noticing how my hands look. When I'm pointing things out on the diagrams, they just look so... wrinkly and uneven to me. It's embarrassing, really. And then, they put some pictures of the training in the company newsletter, and I just couldn't help but feel like my face looked crooked in them. 

But you're exactly right—when I've tried to sort of casually mention it to my coworkers, they always say they don't see what I'm talking about. They say my hands look perfectly fine and that I look nice in the photos. I really don't want to seem vain or like I'm fishing for compliments, you know? I'd hate for them—or you—to think I'm just being silly about getting older. 

It's just hard when I see it so clearly, but no one else does. I really try to just smile and agree with them so I don't make anyone uncomfortable. Am I explaining that right? I hope I'm not making too much of it.

And then, they put some pictures of the training in the company newsletter, and I just couldn't help but feel like my face looked crooked in them. \n\nBut you're exactly right—when I've tried to sort of casually mention it to my coworkers, they always say they don't see what I'm talking about. They say my hands look perfectly fine and that I look nice in the photos. I really don't want to seem vain or like I'm fishing for compliments, you know? I'd hate for them—or you—to think I'm just being silly about getting older. \n\nIt's just hard when I see it so clearly, but no one else does. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I wouldn't call it a *strong* urge, exactly. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over myself! But... I suppose I do have a little routine, just to make sure I'm presentable so I don't bother anyone. 

Before I lead a class or when I go to my senior-center workshop, I'll usually pop into the bathroom to check the mirror a few times. I just put a little extra lotion on my hands to try and smooth them out, and maybe turn my face a few different ways to see how it looks in the light. And... well, sometimes I'll ask a coworker if I look all right, just to be safe. I really try not to bother them too much with it, though! 

Doing all of that does make me run a little behind schedule sometimes, which I feel terrible about because I hate keeping people waiting, but I always make sure I still get myself there and do what I need to do. I hope that doesn't sound too vain or silly, doctor. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?

I wouldn't call it anything too extreme, I promise! \n\nJust... before a training class at work, or when I'm helping out at the senior-center workshops, I might pop into the ladies' room to check the mirror. Maybe just a few times, just to be absolutely sure. I'll put some lotion on my hands so they don't look so dry, and... well, I might turn my face a few different ways in the light, just to see how the angles look and check on that unevenness. Sometimes I'll even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little peace of mind. \n\nI'll admit it does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes, which I always feel terrible about, but I still get myself there and get the job done! I just like to make sure I look neat for everyone. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor? I hope I don't sound too vain admitting all that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have good insight into these concerns about your hands and face, meaning you recognize that your worries might be more intense or frequent than what most people experience?\n\nPatient: Well, um... insight? I'm not entirely sure what you mean by that, doctor. I mean, I try very hard to be reasonable about things. I really do. \n\nI know I worry about it... probably a little more than the other ladies do. Like I said, they tell me they don't see what I see. But to me, it's just so clear when I look down at my hands or see those pictures. So, I suppose I realize that maybe my... my worrying about it is a bit much? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and looking professional for the safety classes. \n\nI don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality or anything like that. I just... I want to look nice. Does that answer your question? I hope I'm giving you the right information. I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about your hands and face ever make you feel noticeably upset, embarrassed, or distressed — maybe even to the point where it’s hard to enjoy things or be yourself around others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm completely miserable or anything like that. I really try to keep a positive attitude! I definitely haven't let it stop me from doing my job or helping out at the senior center. I'd never want to let anyone down. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does bother me a little bit. Like I mentioned, it makes me a little slower getting ready for the training classes, because I'm just trying to make sure I look okay. And, um... lately, I've found myself sort of stepping out of the way when people bring out a camera. I just... I still go! I just... I worry a little more about how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly insecure or silly. I just like to put my best foot forward, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do your worries about your appearance ever go beyond your hands and face, or do they mostly stay focused on those specific areas?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, doctor, goodness no. So those are the only things I really fuss over, just to make sure I look neat and tidy for everyone. I really don't worry about the rest of it at all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything over and over — like checking mirrors, covering them up, or going through the details in your head — to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I certainly wouldn't say I do things over and over in a bad way. I try very hard not to be a bother to anyone or make a fuss! But... I guess I worry a little bit that people might be looking at my hands and thinking I look too old to be doing the trainings, or that they're noticing how crooked my face looks in those pictures. \n\nBut I always make sure to smile and get through the presentation just fine. I really try not to let it show, because I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm not doing a good job. I hope that's a normal thing to feel? I mean, nobody likes having their picture taken, right? I'm probably just being a bit vain, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do these concerns about your hands and face distract you or take up a lot of your time, to the point where it interferes with your daily life or causes you a lot of upset?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no, it certainly hasn't stopped me from doing my job or volunteering! I would never let it get in the way of my responsibilities. \n\nBut... well, I suppose if I'm being completely honest, it does slow me down a little bit when I'm getting ready for the trainings. I just spend a bit more time trying to make sure I look presentable, you know? That's normal for a woman my age, right? \n\nThough, I guess I have started sort of... quietly ducking out of the way when they bring the camera around at work. Just so I don't ruin the picture. And sometimes I catch myself hesitating before going to social get-togethers that I used to just jump right into and enjoy. I just feel a bit shy about it all lately, I suppose. It's really not a huge disruption, I promise, I still get everything done. I hope that's the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about how you see these concerns about your hands and face? Do you find yourself thinking about them often during the day, even when you're not in front of a mirror or about to take a photo?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do think about it a fair bit. I really don't want to sound like I'm obsessed or anything, because I know there are much more important things going on in the world. But... yes, ever since I started helping with those safety trainings, it's just sort of... always there in the back of my mind. \n\nEven when I'm just sitting at my station or driving home, I catch myself thinking about how wrinkly and uneven my hands must have looked when I was pointing at the safety charts. And then my mind just sort of wanders to those newsletter photos, and I start worrying about my face looking crooked again. It's silly, isn't it? Especially since the other ladies swear they don't see it. I just can't seem to shake the thought, though. I try to just push it away and focus on my work, but it keeps popping back up. Is it normal to get stuck on something like that? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these concerns about your hands and face are excessive or unreasonable, even though you try not to let them bother you too much?\n\nPatient: Well, um... excessive? I'm not really sure. I certainly hope I'm not being unreasonable! I really try hard not to be one of those people who makes a mountain out of a molehill. \n\nI mean, I know I shouldn't worry about it as much as I do, and it's probably just me being a little silly. Because I do see the wrinkles and the crookedness, you know? It looks very real to me when I look at those pictures or look down at my hands. But then... well, like I said, the other ladies say they don't see it at all. So I suppose maybe I am making a bit too big a deal out of it? \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm losing my sense of perspective or anything like that. I just... I don't know for sure. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too foolish saying all this.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly—like checking mirrors, trying to cover them up, or going over it in your mind—to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do have a little bit of a routine, just to make sure I'm presentable. I wouldn't call it anything too extreme, I promise! \n\nJust... before a training class at work, or when I'm helping out at the senior-center workshops, I might pop into the ladies' room to check the mirror. Maybe just a few times, just to be absolutely sure. I'll put some lotion on my hands so they don't look so dry, and... well, I might turn my face a few different ways in the light, just to see how the angles look and check on that unevenness. Sometimes I'll even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little peace of mind. \n\nI'll admit it does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes, which I always feel terrible about, but I still get myself there and get the job done! I just like to make sure I look neat for everyone. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor? I hope I don't sound too vain admitting all that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have good insight into these concerns about your hands and face, meaning you recognize that your worries might be more intense or frequent than what most people experience?\n\nPatient: Well, um... insight? I'm not entirely sure what you mean by that, doctor. I mean, I try very hard to be reasonable about things. I really do. \n\nI know I worry about it... probably a little more than the other ladies do. Like I said, they tell me they don't see what I see. But to me, it's just so clear when I look down at my hands or see those pictures. So, I suppose I realize that maybe my... my worrying about it is a bit much? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and looking professional for the safety classes. \n\nI don't think I'm completely out of touch with reality or anything like that. I just... I want to look nice. Does that answer your question? I hope I'm giving you the right information. I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do these worries about your hands and face ever make you feel noticeably upset, embarrassed, or distressed — maybe even to the point where it’s hard to enjoy things or be yourself around others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm completely miserable or anything like that. I really try to keep a positive attitude! I definitely haven't let it stop me from doing my job or helping out at the senior center. I'd never want to let anyone down. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does bother me a little bit. Like I mentioned, it makes me a little slower getting ready for the training classes, because I'm just trying to make sure I look okay. And, um... lately, I've found myself sort of stepping out of the way when people bring out a camera. I just... I don't want to ruin the picture with my face or my hands, you know? \n\nAnd I guess I do hesitate a tiny bit before going to some of the social get-togethers I used to really look forward to. I still go! I just... I worry a little more about how I'm coming across. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly insecure or silly. I just like to put my best foot forward, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do your worries about your appearance ever go beyond your hands and face, or do they mostly stay focused on those specific areas?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, doctor, goodness no. It's really just my hands and my face, like we talked about. I definitely don't sit around nitpicking my whole body or anything like that! I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely obsessed with myself. \n\nIt's just... well, working on the production floor all these years, your hands just take a bit of a beating, you know? And your face is what everyone sees first when you're talking to them, especially when I'm up there doing the safety training. So those are the only things I really fuss over, just to make sure I look neat and tidy for everyone. I really don't worry about the rest of it at all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: When you’re worried about how your hands or face look, do you find yourself doing anything over and over — like checking mirrors, covering them up, or going through the details in your head — to try to feel better or make sure you look okay?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I certainly wouldn't say I do things over and over in a bad way. I try very hard not to be a bother to anyone or make a fuss! But... I suppose when I'm getting ready, especially right before a safety class or one of my workshops at the senior center, I do like to make sure I'm presentable. \n\nSo I might, you know, just pop into the bathroom and check the mirror a few times. And I'll put some extra lotion on my hands, and maybe turn my face a few different ways in the light just to see how everything looks. Sometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just for a little reassurance. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too vain or silly of me. It does make me run a little bit behind schedule sometimes, just taking those extra minutes to double-check, but I always get myself there and get the job done! I'd never let it ruin the class or anything like that. \n\nDoes that sound like what you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if these concerns about your hands and face have ever caused you to avoid certain situations, or made it harder for you to function in your usual roles at work, at home, or with friends?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, um... I know I touched on this a little bit before, but I really want to assure you that I haven't let it stop me from doing my job or my volunteering! I take my responsibilities very seriously, and I would never want to leave anyone in a lurch. \n\nIt's just... well, like I said, it does make me a bit slower getting ready for my training classes. I just need those extra few minutes to make sure everything is... presentable. And I suppose I have started ducking out of photos lately. I just don't want to spoil anyone's nice memories with my hands or my face looking the way they do. \n\nAnd, um, I do find myself hesitating a bit more before going to social events. Things I used to just go to without a second thought... now I just pause and worry a little about how I'll look to everyone else. But I still go! I really try my best not to let it hold me back completely. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not coping well. I really am trying my best to keep up with everything. Does that answer your question, doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the worry about your hands or face made it hard for you to interact with others, or caused you to feel so upset that it was difficult to enjoy being around people?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really wouldn't say it's ever gotten quite that bad. I mean, I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting in a corner somewhere, unable to talk to anyone or just miserable all the time! \n\nI really do enjoy being around people, especially my friends at the senior center and the folks at work.
